OK so now back to 3-hydroxypyruvate:

The CH₂OH is at C3 (which is α to the ketone C2).

So yes, C3 of 3-hydroxypyruvate is the nucleophilic carbon, analogous to C1 of DHAP.

After the reaction, C3 of 3-hydroxypyruvate (= CH₂OH) becomes a new stereocenter CH(OH) bonded to the new carbon.

OK so my earlier analysis was correct. The product has:
- C3 from 3-hydroxypyruvate&#x27;s C3 (CH₂OH → CH(OH)): new stereocenter, configuration = R (from FucA&#x27;s normal selectivity)
- C4 from glyceraldehyde&#x27;s C1 (CHO → CH(OH)): new stereocenter, configuration = S (opposite of what FucA gives with L-lactaldehyde)
- C5 from glyceraldehyde&#x27;s C2 (CHOH): existing stereocenter, configuration = R (D-glyceraldehyde)

Product: (3R,4S,5R)-3,4,5,6-tetrahydroxy-2-oxohexanoate = Option B ✓

I&#x27;m confident in answer B.

Actually, wait. I want to make sure about the C3 configuration.

In FucA&#x27;s normal reaction:

The new stereocenter formed is at what was DHAP C1.

L-fuculose has L-galacto configuration.

So FucA gives R configuration at the carbon derived from DHAP C1.

With 3-hydroxypyruvate as donor (same active site geometry):

With D-glyceraldehyde → C4=S (inverted because the acceptor has opposite configuration)
